# Module map of the TypeScript core (Phase 4) → Rust packages

Generated by `scripts/parity/module-map.mjs`; do not edit. Regenerate with `node scripts/parity/module-map.mjs`; `--check` (run by `pnpm docs:check` and by the `core-parity` job) fails when this file is not what the rules produce, so a new, moved or removed file without a rule change is caught.

Scope: every `src` file of every non-UI TypeScript package (`packages/*` except `web`, which stays TypeScript, D129) and every test file of those packages, listed from `git ls-files`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-phase4-ts-core-to-rust.md` (PR #902; named, not linked, because it is not on `main` yet) §1 and §3 (package scopes). Line counts are raw lines (comments and blank lines included), as in the plan. **The mapping rules are the tables at the top of `scripts/parity/module-map.mjs`** (ordered, first match wins, each with the plan section or the evidence); a file no rule matches is `unassigned`, never guessed.

## Dispositions and modes

| Disposition | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `port` | behaviour the Rust core must reproduce; the package's parity gates apply |
| `fold` | becomes a Rust idiom or an existing Rust crate; the note names the target |
| `drop` | not ported: deleted by the cut (P4-90) or by an owner decision; the note says why |
| `engine` | belongs to the memory engine (RE programme), not to plur1bus-core |
| `stays-TS` | stays TypeScript: the API and import for 0.1 (Q1), the module SDK, build-time and browser code, tooling |
| `unassigned` | no rule matches; visible on purpose, a rule (or an owner answer) is needed |

| Mode | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `V` | vectors: an exporter records pure-function results, Rust reads them (scripts/parity/) |
| `T` | transcripts: RPC traffic recorded from the TS core, replayed against the Rust core (tests/core-parity/) |
| `S` | system: tests/system on PLUR1BUS_CORE_IMPL=ts and rust |
| `W` | wire: HTTP/WebSocket exchanges recorded into fixtures, served by a Rust fake server (P4-03) |
| `B` | stores: the same SQLite file written by TS, read and written by Rust, read by TS again |

A test file with no mode carries a reason instead (test support, a package that stays TypeScript, a test of a retired mechanism). `V,T` means both modes apply.
